@container/columns-container relative mt-8 prose prose-gray dark:prose-invert [contain:inline-size] isolate empty:hidden mb-14" data-page-title="Intercept and control agent behavior with hooks" data-page-href="/en/agent-sdk/hooks" id="content"><span data-as="p">Hooks are callback functions that run your code in response to agent events, like a tool being called, a session starting, or execution stopping. With hooks, you can:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Block dangerous operations</strong> before they execute, like destructive shell commands or unauthorized file access</li>
<li><strong>Log and audit</strong> tool calls for compliance, debugging, or analytics</li>
<li><strong>Transform inputs and outputs</strong> to sanitize data, inject credentials, or redirect file paths</li>
<li><strong>Require human approval</strong> for sensitive actions like database writes or API calls</li>
<li><strong>Track session lifecycle</strong> to manage state, clean up resources, or send notifications</li>
</ul>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="how-hooks-work" aria-labelledby="_R_59deehkd74t9_"><a href="#how-hooks-work" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: How hooks work"></a><span id="_R_59deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">How hooks work</span></h2>
<div role="list" class="steps ml-3.5 mt-10 mb-6"><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-gray-200/70 dark:bg-white/10" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">1</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">An event fires</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">Something happens during agent execution and the SDK fires an event: a tool is about to be called (<code>PreToolUse</code>), a tool returned a result (<code>PostToolUse</code>), a subagent started or stopped, the agent is idle, or execution finished. See the <a href="#available-hooks" class="link">full list of events</a>.</span></div></div></div><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-gray-200/70 dark:bg-white/10" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">2</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">The SDK collects registered hooks</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">The SDK checks for hooks registered for that event type. This includes callback hooks you pass in <code>options.hooks</code> and shell command hooks from settings files when the corresponding <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#settingsource" class="link"><code>settingSources</code></a> or <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#settingsource" class="link"><code>setting_sources</code></a> entry is enabled, which it is for default <code>query()</code> options.</span></div></div></div><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-gray-200/70 dark:bg-white/10" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">3</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">Matchers filter which hooks run</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">If a hook has a <a href="#matchers" class="link"><code>matcher</code></a> pattern (like <code>&quot;Write|Edit&quot;</code>), the SDK tests it against the event’s target (for example, the tool name). Hooks without a matcher run for every event of that type.</span></div></div></div><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-gray-200/70 dark:bg-white/10" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">4</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">Callback functions execute</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">Each matching hook’s <a href="#callback-functions" class="link">callback function</a> receives input about what’s happening: the tool name, its arguments, the session ID, and other event-specific details.</span></div></div></div><div role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-transparent bg-linear-to-b from-gray-200 dark:from-white/10 via-80% to-transparent [.step-container:has([data-component-part=step-content]:empty)_&amp;]:hidden" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">5</div></div></div><div class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><p class="mt-2 font-semibold prose dark:prose-invert text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-200" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">Your callback returns a decision</p><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">After performing any operations (logging, API calls, validation), your callback returns an <a href="#outputs" class="link">output object</a> that tells the agent what to do: allow the operation, block it, modify the input, or inject context into the conversation.</span></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">This example registers a <code>PreToolUse</code> hook (step 1) with a <code>&quot;Write|Edit&quot;</code> matcher (step 3) so the callback fires for the Write and Edit tools. When triggered, the callback receives the tool’s input (step 4), checks if the file path targets a <code>.env</code> file, and returns <code>permissionDecision: &quot;deny&quot;</code> to block the operation (step 5):</span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#C586C0">  if</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> (</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">message</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">type</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> ===</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178"> &quot;assistant&quot;</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> ||</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE"> message</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">type</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> ===</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178"> &quot;result&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">) {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">    console</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">log</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">(</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">message</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">);</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">  }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">}</span></span>
</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">The hook denies a Write or Edit call whose <code>file_path</code> ends in <code>/.env</code>, in any letter case. On Windows, <code>file_path</code> uses backslashes, such as <code>C:\project\.env</code>, and this hook doesn’t block a Write or Edit call to <code>C:\project\.env</code>.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Warning" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-yellow-200 bg-yellow-50 dark:border-yellow-900 dark:bg-yellow-600/20 [&amp;_[data-component-part=&#x27;callout-icon&#x27;]]:mt-px" data-callout-type="warning"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="flex-none size-5 text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutWarning"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-yellow-800 dark:text-yellow-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">The <code>Write|Edit</code> matcher limits this hook to the Write and Edit tools. If Claude writes <code>.env</code> with a Bash command, the hook doesn’t run. To block Bash writes to a file where <a href="/docs/en/sandboxing" class="link">the sandbox runs</a>, see the sandbox configuration for <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#sandbox-configuration" class="link">Python</a> or <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sandbox-configuration" class="link">TypeScript</a>.</span></div></div>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="available-hooks" aria-labelledby="_R_h9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#available-hooks" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Available hooks"></a><span id="_R_h9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Available hooks</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">The SDK provides hooks for different stages of agent execution. Some hooks are available in both SDKs, while others are TypeScript-only.</span>

<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="configure-hooks" aria-labelledby="_R_n9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#configure-hooks" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Configure hooks"></a><span id="_R_n9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Configure hooks</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">To configure a hook, pass it in the <code>hooks</code> field of your agent options (<code>ClaudeAgentOptions</code> in Python, the <code>options</code> object in TypeScript). This snippet assumes you have already defined a hook callback, like <code>protect_env_files</code> in Python or <code>protectEnvFiles</code> in TypeScript from the example above:</span>

</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div><div data-hidden="" data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" hidden="" id="base-ui-_R_a8r9deehkd74t9_" role="tabpanel" tabindex="0" inert="" data-index="-1" class="w-full min-w-full max-w-full h-full max-h-full relative focus-visible:outline-hidden before:content-[&#x27;&#x27;] before:absolute before:inset-0 before:z-10 before:pointer-events-none before:rounded-xt focus-visible:before:outline focus-visible:before:outline-1 focus-visible:before:-outline-offset-2 focus-visible:before:outline-primary dark:focus-visible:before:outline-primary-light data-hidden:hidden [&amp;_[data-component-part=code-block-root]]:rounded-xt!"><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px;font-variant-ligatures:none;height:100%;background-color:#ffffff;--shiki-dark-bg:#0B0C0E" data-component-part="code-block-root" class="w-0 min-w-full max-w-full h-full dark:bg-codeblock text-sm leading-6 transition-[height] duration-300 ease-in-out code-block-background **:ring-0 **:outline-0 **:focus:ring-0 **:focus:outline-0 rounded-xt bg-white"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_1q8r9deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="0" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] py-3.5 px-4 focus-visible:outline-1! <span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">  prompt:</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178"> &quot;Your prompt&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">,</span></span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">      PreToolUse:</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> [{ </span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">matcher:</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178"> &quot;Bash&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">, </span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">hooks:</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> [</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">myCallback</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">] }]</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">    }</span></span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">}</span></span>

<span data-as="p">The <code>hooks</code> option is a dictionary in Python or an object in TypeScript, where:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Keys</strong>: <a href="#available-hooks" class="link">hook event names</a> such as <code>&#x27;PreToolUse&#x27;</code>, <code>&#x27;PostToolUse&#x27;</code>, and <code>&#x27;Stop&#x27;</code></li>
<li><strong>Values</strong>: arrays of <a href="#matchers" class="link">matchers</a>, each containing an optional filter pattern and your <a href="#callback-functions" class="link">callback functions</a></li>
</ul>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="matchers" aria-labelledby="_R_119deehkd74t9_"><a href="#matchers" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Matchers"></a><span id="_R_119deehkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Matchers</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Use matchers to filter when your callbacks fire. The <code>matcher</code> field matches against a different value depending on the hook event type. For example, tool-based hooks match against the tool name, while <code>Notification</code> hooks match against the notification type.</span>
<span data-as="p">SDK matchers follow the same rules as <a href="/docs/en/hooks#matcher-patterns" class="link">matchers in settings files</a>. That section documents the exact-string and regular-expression evaluation paths, their version requirements, and the matcher values for each event type.</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div data-id="base-ui-_R_179deehkd74t9_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" role="region" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th>Option</th><th>Type</th><th>Default</th><th>Description</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>matcher</code></td><td><code>string</code></td><td><code>undefined</code></td><td>Pattern matched against the event’s filter field, following the <a href="/docs/en/hooks#matcher-patterns" class="link">rules for matchers in settings files</a>. For tool hooks, that field is the tool name. Built-in tools include <code>Bash</code>, <code>Read</code>, <code>Write</code>, <code>Edit</code>, <code>Glob</code>, <code>Grep</code>, <code>WebFetch</code>, <code>Agent</code>, and others. See <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#tool-input-types" class="link">Tool Input Types</a> for the full list. MCP tools use the pattern <code>mcp__&lt;server&gt;__&lt;action&gt;</code>, where <code>&lt;server&gt;</code> is the key you use in the <code>mcpServers</code> configuration, spelled as the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/mcp#tool-naming-convention" class="link">tool naming convention</a> describes.</td></tr><tr><td><code>hooks</code></td><td><code>HookCallback[]</code></td><td data-numeric="true">-</td><td>Required. Array of callback functions to execute when the pattern matches</td></tr><tr><td><code>timeout</code></td><td><code>number</code></td><td><code>undefined</code></td><td>Timeout in seconds. When omitted, Claude Code applies the <a href="#hook-timeout" class="link">event’s default timeout</a>. Your SDK callbacks follow the <code>command</code> hook defaults</td></tr><tr><td><code>onFailure</code></td><td><code>&quot;continue&quot; | &quot;block&quot;</code></td><td><code>undefined</code></td><td>What a failed callback does. Set <code>&quot;block&quot;</code> to <a href="#block-the-action-when-a-callback-fails" class="link">block the action when a callback fails</a>. In Python, use <code>on_failure</code></td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">Use the <code>matcher</code> pattern to target specific tools whenever possible. With a <code>&#x27;Bash&#x27;</code> matcher, your callbacks run for Bash commands. On Claude Code v2.1.314 or later, they also run on <code>PreToolUse</code> and <code>PermissionRequest</code> for Monitor commands, which they <a href="/docs/en/hooks#match-monitor-commands" class="link">can deny but not approve</a>. With no matcher, they run for every occurrence of the event.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="callback-functions" aria-labelledby="_R_1b9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#callback-functions" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Callback functions"></a><span id="_R_1b9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Callback functions</span></h3>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="inputs" aria-labelledby="_R_1d9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#inputs" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Inputs"></a><span id="_R_1d9deehkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Inputs</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">Every hook callback receives three arguments:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Input data:</strong> a typed object containing event details. Each hook type has its own input shape. For example, <code>PreToolUseHookInput</code> includes <code>tool_name</code> and <code>tool_input</code>, while <code>NotificationHookInput</code> includes <code>message</code>. See the full type definitions in the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#hookinput" class="link">TypeScript</a> and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#hookinput" class="link">Python</a> SDK references.<!-- -->
<ul>
<li>All hook inputs share <code>session_id</code>, <code>cwd</code>, and <code>hook_event_name</code>.</li>
<li><code>agent_id</code> and <code>agent_type</code> are populated when the hook fires inside a subagent. In TypeScript, these are on the base hook input and available to all hook types. In Python, they are optional fields on <code>PreToolUse</code>, <code>PostToolUse</code>, <code>PostToolUseFailure</code>, and <code>PermissionRequest</code>, and required fields on <code>SubagentStart</code> and <code>SubagentStop</code>.</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li><strong>Tool use ID</strong> (<code>str | None</code> / <code>string | undefined</code>): correlates <code>PreToolUse</code> and <code>PostToolUse</code> events for the same tool call.</li>
<li><strong>Context:</strong> in TypeScript, contains a <code>signal</code> property (<code>AbortSignal</code>) for cancellation. In Python, this argument is reserved for future use.</li>
</ul>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="outputs" aria-labelledby="_R_1j9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#outputs" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Outputs"></a><span id="_R_1j9deehkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Outputs</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">Your callback returns an object with two categories of fields:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Top-level fields</strong> are accepted on every event: <code>systemMessage</code> shows a message to the user, and <code>continue</code> (<code>continue_</code> in Python) determines whether the agent keeps running after this hook. Some events discard them or deliver them elsewhere. Each <a href="/docs/en/hooks#hook-events" class="link">event’s section</a> on the hooks page says where they land.</li>
<li><strong><code>hookSpecificOutput</code></strong> controls the current operation. The fields you set inside depend on the hook event type:<!-- -->
<ul>
<li>For <code>PreToolUse</code> hooks, this is where you set <code>permissionDecision</code> (<code>&quot;allow&quot;</code>, <code>&quot;deny&quot;</code>, <code>&quot;ask&quot;</code>, or <code>&quot;defer&quot;</code>), <code>permissionDecisionReason</code>, and <code>updatedInput</code>. If you return <code>&quot;defer&quot;</code>, the turn ends with a result message whose <code>stop_reason</code> is <code>&quot;tool_deferred&quot;</code>, so you can <a href="/docs/en/hooks#defer-a-tool-call-for-later" class="link">resume the call later</a>.</li>
<li>For <code>PostToolUse</code> hooks, you can set <code>additionalContext</code> to append information to the tool result. To replace the tool’s output before Claude sees it, set <code>updatedToolOutput</code>, which works for built-in and MCP tools in both SDKs. The older <code>updatedMCPToolOutput</code> field replaces MCP tool output only.</li>
<li>In the TypeScript SDK, a <code>PostToolUse</code> callback can also return <code>classifierContext</code>, a short note about the tool call’s result for the <a href="/docs/en/permission-modes#eliminate-prompts-with-auto-mode" class="link">auto mode</a> permission classifier. Because your callback runs in your application’s own process, the classifier may weigh a user statement you relay in the note as user intent. The field requires TypeScript Agent SDK v0.3.236 or later. <a href="/docs/en/hooks#annotate-a-result-for-the-auto-mode-classifier" class="link">Annotate a result for the auto mode classifier</a> covers the length cap, the synchronous-only rule, and what not to put in the note.</li>
</ul>
</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">For <code>PreToolUse</code>, return <code>{}</code> to make no decision, so the normal <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions#how-permissions-are-evaluated" class="link">permission flow</a> applies. SDK callback hooks use the same JSON output format as <a href="/docs/en/hooks#json-output" class="link">Claude Code shell command hooks</a>, which documents every field and event-specific option. For the SDK type definitions, see the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#synchookjsonoutput" class="link">TypeScript</a> and <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#synchookjsonoutput" class="link">Python</a> SDK references.</span>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">When multiple <code>PreToolUse</code> hooks return different <a href="/docs/en/hooks#pretooluse-decision-control" class="link">decisions</a>, <code>deny</code> takes priority over <code>defer</code>, which takes priority over <code>ask</code>, which takes priority over <code>allow</code>.</span></div></div>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="asynchronous-output" aria-labelledby="_R_1t9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#asynchronous-output" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Asynchronous output"></a><span id="_R_1t9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Asynchronous output</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">By default, the agent waits for your hook to return before proceeding. If your hook performs a side effect, such as logging or sending a webhook, and doesn’t need to influence the agent’s behavior, you can return an async output instead. This tells the agent to continue immediately without waiting for the hook to finish. In this snippet, <code>send_to_logging_service</code> in Python and <code>sendToLoggingService</code> in TypeScript stand in for any logging function you define:</span>
<div data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" class="code-group p-0.5 mt-5 mb-8 flex flex-col not-prose relative rounded-2xl border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 The agent proceeds without waiting. In Python, use <code>async_</code> to avoid the reserved keyword.</td></tr><tr><td><code>asyncTimeout</code></td><td><code>number</code></td><td>Optional timeout in milliseconds for the background operation</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">Async outputs can’t block, modify, or inject context into the operation since the agent has already moved on. Use them only for side effects like logging, metrics, or notifications.</span></div></div>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="block-the-action-when-a-callback-fails" aria-labelledby="_R_279deehkd74t9_"><a href="#block-the-action-when-a-callback-fails" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Block the action when a callback fails"></a><span id="_R_279deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Block the action when a callback fails</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">On most events, if a callback throws or returns output that fails validation, the action goes ahead and your app receives an <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkinformationalmessage" class="link"><code>SDKInformationalMessage</code></a> warning whose <code>tag</code> is <code>hook_callback_failed</code>. A <code>PreToolUse</code> guard that crashes then lets the tool call reach the normal permission flow. A callback that returns nothing, such as a path with no <code>return</code>, also counts as failing. In Python, read <code>tag</code> from <code>SystemMessage.data</code>.</span>
<span data-as="p">To block the action instead, set <code>onFailure: &quot;block&quot;</code> in TypeScript or <code>on_failure=&quot;block&quot;</code> in Python on the matcher. Requires TypeScript Agent SDK v0.3.317 or later, or Python Agent SDK v0.2.177 or later. This example blocks a Bash call when <code>check_policy</code> in Python or <code>checkPolicy</code> in TypeScript fails:</span>
<div data-orientation="horizontal" data-activation-direction="none" class="code-group p-0.5 mt-5 mb-8 flex flex-col not-prose relative rounded-2xl border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 h-full children:my-0! children:shadow-none! children:bg-transparent!"><div class="font-mono whitespace-pre flex-none text-sm h-full leading-6" data-component-part="code-group-tab-content"><pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light-default dark-plus" style="background-color:#ffffff;--shiki-dark-bg:#0B0C0E;color:#1f2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4" language="typescript"><code language="typescript" numberOfLines="7"><span class="line"><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#C586C0">import</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#C586C0"> type</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> { </span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">Options</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> } </span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#C586C0">from</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178"> &quot;@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">;</span></span>
<span class="line"></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#569CD6">const</span><span style="color:#0550AE;--shiki-dark:#4FC1FF"> options</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">:</span><span style="color:#953800;--shiki-dark:#4EC9B0"> Options</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> =</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">  hooks:</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">    PreToolUse:</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> [{ </span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">matcher:</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178"> &quot;Bash&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">, </span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">hooks:</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> [</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">checkPolicy</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">], </span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">onFailure:</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178"> &quot;block&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> }]</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">  }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">};</span></span>
</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">To test it, make the callback raise an error and ask Claude to run a Bash command. The tool result includes <code>failed; blocking because onFailure is &quot;block&quot;</code>, and Claude doesn’t see your error text.</span>
<span data-as="p">Return <code>{}</code> when the callback has no opinion. With <code>&quot;block&quot;</code> set, a path with no <code>return</code> blocks the action. A callback that catches its own error and returns <code>{}</code> isn’t failing, so let the error propagate from a guard.</span>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="events-that-behave-differently" aria-labelledby="_R_2j9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#events-that-behave-differently" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Events that behave differently"></a><span id="_R_2j9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Events that behave differently</span></h4>
<span data-as="p">These events don’t always block with <code>&quot;block&quot;</code> set:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong><code>Stop</code>, <code>SubagentStop</code>, <code>TaskCompleted</code>, and <code>TeammateIdle</code></strong>: these events ignore the option, so the action goes ahead.</li>
<li><strong><code>PermissionRequest</code></strong>: the block counts only if it arrives before your <code>canUseTool</code> callback answers.</li>
<li><strong><code>PostToolUse</code></strong>: the tool has already run, so Claude receives the failure as feedback.</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">A callback that exceeds its timeout follows <a href="#hook-timeout" class="link">Hook timeout</a>. With <code>&quot;block&quot;</code> set, a timeout also blocks <code>PreCompact</code>, <code>Elicitation</code>, and <code>WorktreeCreate</code>.</span>
<h4 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="startup-errors-for-onfailure" aria-labelledby="_R_2r9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#startup-errors-for-onfailure" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Startup errors for onFailure"></a><span id="_R_2r9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Startup errors for <code>onFailure</code></span></h4>
<span data-as="p">With <code>&quot;block&quot;</code> set, the query fails at startup and sends no prompt when it can’t honor the option:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Claude Code doesn’t support the option</strong>: the error includes <code>does not honor</code>. Update Claude Code, or remove the option.</li>
<li><strong>The session was already running and didn’t register your hooks</strong>: the error includes <code>did not register your hooks</code>. Start a new session.</li>
</ul>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="examples" aria-labelledby="_R_319deehkd74t9_"><a href="#examples" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Examples"></a><span id="_R_319deehkd74t9_" class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Examples</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Several examples in this section show only the callback function. To run one, register the callback under the matching event in the <code>hooks</code> field of your options, as shown in <a href="#configure-hooks" class="link">Configure hooks</a>.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="modify-tool-input" aria-labelledby="_R_359deehkd74t9_"><a href="#modify-tool-input" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Modify tool input"></a><span id="_R_359deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Modify tool input</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">This example intercepts Write tool calls and rewrites the <code>file_path</code> argument to prepend <code>/sandbox</code>. The callback returns <code>updatedInput</code> with the modified path and <code>permissionDecision: &#x27;allow&#x27;</code> to auto-approve the rewritten operation:</span>

<div role="note" aria-label="Note" class="callout my-4 px-5 py-4 overflow-hidden rounded-2xl flex gap-3 border border-blue-200 bg-blue-50 dark:border-blue-900 dark:bg-blue-600/20" data-callout-type="note"><div class="mt-0.5 w-4" data-component-part="callout-icon"><svg aria-hidden="true" width="18" height="18" stroke-width="1.5" class="size-4 text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300"><use href="#mi-mint-1-CalloutNote"></use></svg></div><div class="text-sm prose dark:prose-invert min-w-0 w-full [&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-light dark:[&amp;_kbd]:bg-background-dark [&amp;_code]:text-current! [&amp;_kbd]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:text-current! [&amp;_a]:border-current [&amp;_strong]:text-current! text-blue-800 dark:text-blue-300" data-component-part="callout-content"><span data-as="p">Pair <code>updatedInput</code> with <code>permissionDecision: &#x27;allow&#x27;</code> to auto-approve the modified input, or <code>permissionDecision: &#x27;ask&#x27;</code> to show it to the user. If you omit <code>permissionDecision</code>, the modified input still applies and flows through the normal permission evaluation. With <code>&#x27;defer&#x27;</code>, <code>updatedInput</code> is ignored. Always return a new object rather than mutating the original <code>tool_input</code>.</span></div></div>
<span data-as="p">To confirm the redirect, set the prefix to a path you can write to, such as <code>./sandbox</code> or <code>/tmp/sandbox</code> (macOS doesn’t allow creating a root-level <code>/sandbox</code> directory), then ask the agent to write a file: the Write tool’s result in the message stream names the path with your sandbox prefix rather than the one Claude requested.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="add-context-and-block-a-tool" aria-labelledby="_R_3f9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#add-context-and-block-a-tool" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Add context and block a tool"></a><span id="_R_3f9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Add context and block a tool</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">This example denies tool calls whose <code>file_path</code> starts with <code>/etc</code> and explains why to both the model and the user:</span>
<ul>
<li><code>permissionDecision: &#x27;deny&#x27;</code> stops the tool call.</li>
<li><code>permissionDecisionReason</code> tells the model why.</li>
<li><code>systemMessage</code> shows the user what happened.</li>
</ul>

<span data-as="p">To confirm the block, register the callback under <code>PreToolUse</code> with a <code>Write|Edit</code> matcher and ask the agent to create a file under <code>/etc</code>: the Write tool’s result in the message stream contains <code>Writing to /etc is not allowed</code>, and no file is created.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="auto-approve-specific-tools" aria-labelledby="_R_3p9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#auto-approve-specific-tools" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Auto-approve specific tools"></a><span id="_R_3p9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Auto-approve specific tools</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">By default, the agent may prompt for permission before using certain tools. This example auto-approves read-only filesystem tools (Read, Glob, Grep) by returning <code>permissionDecision: &#x27;allow&#x27;</code>, letting them run without user confirmation, apart from reads from <a href="/docs/en/permissions#network-paths" class="link">network paths</a>, while leaving all other tools subject to normal permission checks:</span>

<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="register-multiple-hooks" aria-labelledby="_R_3v9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#register-multiple-hooks" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Register multiple hooks"></a><span id="_R_3v9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Register multiple hooks</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">When an event fires, the matching hooks run in parallel. For permission decisions, the most restrictive result applies. One hook’s <code>deny</code> blocks the tool call. Because completion order is non-deterministic, write each hook to act independently rather than relying on another hook having run first.</span>
<span data-as="p">The example below registers three independent checks, each with an <a href="/docs/en/hooks#matcher-patterns" class="link">omitted matcher</a>. The hook names in it, such as <code>audit_logger</code> in Python or <code>auditLogger</code> in TypeScript, stand in for callbacks you define:</span>

</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div></div></div>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="filter-with-multi-tool-matchers" aria-labelledby="_R_479deehkd74t9_"><a href="#filter-with-multi-tool-matchers" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Filter with multi-tool matchers"></a><span id="_R_479deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Filter with multi-tool matchers</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Use multi-tool matchers to share one callback across related tools. This example registers three matchers with different scopes, and each hook it names stands in for a callback you define:</span>
<ul>
<li>A pipe-separated exact list (<code>Write|Edit|NotebookEdit</code>) triggers <code>file_security_hook</code> for the Write, Edit, and NotebookEdit tools.</li>
<li>A regex (<code>^mcp__</code>) triggers <code>mcp_audit_hook</code> for any MCP tool whose name starts with <code>mcp__</code>.</li>
<li>An omitted matcher triggers <code>global_logger</code> without filtering by tool name.</li>
</ul>

<span data-as="p">To confirm the hook fires, register the callback and ask the agent to delegate a small task to a subagent, such as listing the files in the current directory: when the subagent finishes, the callback prints the <code>[SUBAGENT] Completed:</code> lines with the subagent’s ID and transcript path.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="make-http-requests-from-hooks" aria-labelledby="_R_4n9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#make-http-requests-from-hooks" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Make HTTP requests from hooks"></a><span id="_R_4n9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Make HTTP requests from hooks</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Hooks can perform asynchronous operations like HTTP requests. Catch errors inside your hook instead of letting them propagate.</span>
<span data-as="p">This example sends a webhook after each tool completes, logging which tool ran and when. The hook catches errors from a failed webhook:</span>

</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">To confirm the hook fires, point the webhook URL at an endpoint you can watch and send a prompt that uses a tool: the hook sends a POST with the tool name and timestamp after each tool completes.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="forward-notifications-to-slack" aria-labelledby="_R_519deehkd74t9_"><a href="#forward-notifications-to-slack" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Forward notifications to Slack"></a><span id="_R_519deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Forward notifications to Slack</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Use <code>Notification</code> hooks to receive system notifications from the agent and forward them to external services. In SDK sessions, Claude Code runs this hook for the following notification types:</span>
<ul>
<li><a href="/docs/en/hooks#notification" class="link"><code>permission_prompt</code></a> once a permission request has waited about six seconds on your <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/user-input" class="link"><code>canUseTool</code> callback</a>. Requires TypeScript Agent SDK v0.3.233 or later, or Python Agent SDK v0.2.139 or later</li>
<li><code>elicitation_complete</code> and <code>elicitation_response</code> for user-prompt elicitation flows</li>
<li><code>plugin_notification</code> when a <a href="/docs/en/plugins/mods/api#show-something-without-starting-a-turn" class="link">mod</a> calls <code>$.ui.notify</code></li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Claude Code emits the other types, such as <code>idle_prompt</code>, <code>auth_success</code>, and <code>elicitation_dialog</code>, from interactive UI that SDK sessions don’t run.</span>
<span data-as="p">Each notification includes a <code>message</code> field with a human-readable description and optionally a <code>title</code>.</span>
<span data-as="p">This example forwards every notification to a Slack channel. It requires a <a href="https://docs.slack.dev/messaging/sending-messages-using-incoming-webhooks/" class="link" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">Slack incoming webhook URL</a>, which you create by adding an app to your Slack workspace and enabling incoming webhooks:</span>

<span data-as="p">When a <code>Notification</code> event fires, the hook posts the notification’s <code>message</code>, prefixed with <code>Agent status:</code>, to the channel your webhook targets.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="fix-common-issues" aria-labelledby="_R_5h9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#fix-common-issues" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Fix common issues"></a><span id="_R_5h9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Fix common issues</span></h2>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="hook-not-firing" aria-labelledby="_R_5j9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#hook-not-firing" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Hook not firing"></a><span id="_R_5j9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Hook not firing</span></h3>
<ul>
<li>Verify the hook event name is correct and case-sensitive (<code>PreToolUse</code>, not <code>preToolUse</code>)</li>
<li>Check that your matcher pattern matches the tool name exactly</li>
<li>Ensure the hook is under the correct event type in <code>options.hooks</code></li>
<li>For non-tool hooks that support matchers, like <code>Notification</code> and <code>SubagentStop</code>, matchers match against different fields, and <code>Stop</code> ignores matchers entirely (see <a href="/docs/en/hooks#matcher-patterns" class="link">matcher patterns</a>)</li>
<li>Hooks may not fire when the agent hits the <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#claudeagentoptions" class="link"><code>max_turns</code></a> limit because the session ends before hooks can execute</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="matcher-not-filtering-as-expected" aria-labelledby="_R_5n9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#matcher-not-filtering-as-expected" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Matcher not filtering as expected"></a><span id="_R_5n9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Matcher not filtering as expected</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Matchers only match tool names, not file paths or other arguments. To filter by file path, check <code>tool_input.file_path</code> inside your hook:</span>
<div class="code-block mt-5 mb-8 not-prose rounded-2xl relative group min-w-0 print:print-color-exact text-gray-950 dark:text-gray-50 codeblock-light border border-gray-950/10 dark:border-white/10 <h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="hook-timeout" aria-labelledby="_R_5t9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#hook-timeout" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Hook timeout"></a><span id="_R_5t9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Hook timeout</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Claude Code runs each callback with a timeout, which you set in seconds with the <code>timeout</code> field on its <code>HookMatcher</code>. When you don’t set one, Claude Code uses the event’s default: 600 seconds for most events, 30 seconds for <code>UserPromptSubmit</code>, <code>PreModelSwitch</code>, and <code>PostModelSwitch</code>, and 10 seconds for <code>MessageDisplay</code>. Claude Code runs <code>SessionEnd</code> callbacks during shutdown under the shorter <a href="/docs/en/hooks#sessionend-input" class="link">SessionEnd timeout budget</a>, 1.5 seconds by default.</span>
<span data-as="p">When a callback exceeds its timeout, Claude Code cancels it and discards its output, and the session continues rather than hanging. What happens next depends on the event:</span>
<ul>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><code>PreToolUse</code>: Claude Code blocks the tool call. If every other hook returned nothing or only an <code>allow</code> decision, the tool result tells Claude the hook didn’t respond before its timeout, and the turn continues. If another of your hooks already denied the call, its deny still reaches Claude: the tool result includes that hook’s reason, and the timeout text can follow it.</span>
<span data-as="p">Before v2.1.210, Claude Code reported the timeout to Claude as a user rejection, which made unattended sessions stop and wait for input.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><code>PostToolUse</code> and <code>PostToolUseFailure</code>: Claude Code keeps the tool result and the turn continues.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><code>UserPromptSubmit</code> and <a href="/docs/en/hooks#userpromptexpansion" class="link"><code>UserPromptExpansion</code></a>: Claude Code blocks the prompt with a message naming the hook and the timeout, and the session continues. Before v2.1.208, Claude Code ended the query with <code>error_during_execution</code> when a callback on these events timed out.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><code>Stop</code> and <code>SubagentStop</code>: the timed-out callback counts as returning no decision. The agent or subagent stops as if that callback had allowed it, and a decision from your other hooks on the event still applies. Before Claude Code v2.1.273, a timed-out <code>Stop</code> or <code>SubagentStop</code> callback counted as a failed hook run, and Claude Code discarded the decisions of your other hooks on the event.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><code>SessionStart</code>: the timed-out callback counts as returning no output, and the session continues with the output of your other <code>SessionStart</code> hooks.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p"><code>PreModelSwitch</code>: Claude Code blocks the model switch. A hook that doesn’t answer hasn’t approved the switch.</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p">Other events, such as <code>Notification</code>, <code>PreCompact</code>, and <code>PostModelSwitch</code>: Claude Code logs the failure and continues. With <a href="#block-the-action-when-a-callback-fails" class="link"><code>onFailure: &quot;block&quot;</code></a>, a timeout on <code>PreCompact</code>, <code>Elicitation</code>, or <code>WorktreeCreate</code> blocks the action.</span>
</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">The first time a <code>Stop</code> or <code>SessionStart</code> callback times out in the main session, Claude Code also adds an <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkinformationalmessage" class="link"><code>SDKInformationalMessage</code></a> to the message stream saying the app driving the session didn’t respond. Later timeouts don’t repeat that message while your app stays unresponsive.</span>
<span data-as="p">If you interrupt the query while a callback is pending, Claude Code cancels the pending tool call. Before v2.1.208, the tool call could still proceed if you interrupted during a pending <code>PreToolUse</code> callback.</span>
<span data-as="p">If your callback needs more time, set a higher <code>timeout</code> on its <code>HookMatcher</code>. In TypeScript, use the <code>AbortSignal</code> from the third callback argument to handle cancellation gracefully when the timeout fires.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="tool-blocked-unexpectedly" aria-labelledby="_R_6b9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#tool-blocked-unexpectedly" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Tool blocked unexpectedly"></a><span id="_R_6b9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Tool blocked unexpectedly</span></h3>
<ul>
<li>Check all <code>PreToolUse</code> hooks for <code>permissionDecision: &#x27;deny&#x27;</code> returns</li>
<li>Add logging to your hooks to see what <code>permissionDecisionReason</code> they’re returning</li>
<li>Verify matcher patterns aren’t too broad: an empty matcher matches all tools</li>
<li>If the tool result says a hook <code>returned updatedInput that failed schema validation</code> or <code>returned updatedInput that the tool refused</code>, fix that hook’s <code>updatedInput</code>. Include the unchanged <code>tool_input</code> fields, as in <a href="#modify-tool-input" class="link">Modify tool input</a>, because <code>updatedInput</code> replaces the whole input.</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="modified-input-not-applied" aria-labelledby="_R_6f9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#modified-input-not-applied" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Modified input not applied"></a><span id="_R_6f9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Modified input not applied</span></h3>
<ul>
<li>
<span data-as="p">Ensure <code>updatedInput</code> is inside <code>hookSpecificOutput</code>, not at the top level:</span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">  hookSpecificOutput:</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">    hookEventName:</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178"> &quot;PreToolUse&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">,</span></span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">    updatedInput:</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> { </span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">command:</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178"> &quot;new command&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">  }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">};</span></span>
</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" style="--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E"></div></div>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p">Don’t pair <code>updatedInput</code> with <code>permissionDecision: &#x27;defer&#x27;</code>, which drops the modified input. Omitting <code>permissionDecision</code> is fine: the modified input still applies through the normal permission evaluation. You can also return <code>&#x27;allow&#x27;</code> to auto-approve the modified input or <code>&#x27;ask&#x27;</code> to show it to the user for approval</span>
</li>
<li>
<span data-as="p">Include <code>hookEventName</code> in <code>hookSpecificOutput</code> to identify which hook type the output is for</span>
</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="session-hooks-not-available-in-python" aria-labelledby="_R_6j9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#session-hooks-not-available-in-python" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Session hooks not available in Python"></a><span id="_R_6j9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Session hooks not available in Python</span></h3>
<span data-as="p"><code>SessionStart</code> and <code>SessionEnd</code> can be registered as SDK callback hooks in TypeScript, but aren’t available in the Python SDK because its <code>HookEvent</code> type omits them. In Python, they are only available as <a href="/docs/en/hooks#hook-events" class="link">shell command hooks</a> defined in settings files such as <code>.claude/settings.json</code>. Which settings files your SDK application loads depends on <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python#settingsource" class="link"><code>setting_sources</code></a> or <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#settingsource" class="link"><code>settingSources</code></a>. If you set that option, include the source that holds the hooks:</span>

<span data-as="p">To run initialization logic as a Python SDK callback instead, use the first message from <code>client.receive_response()</code> as your trigger.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="subagent-permission-prompts-multiplying" aria-labelledby="_R_6r9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#subagent-permission-prompts-multiplying" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Subagent permission prompts multiplying"></a><span id="_R_6r9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Subagent permission prompts multiplying</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">When spawning multiple subagents, each one may request permissions separately for its own tool calls. To avoid repeated prompts, use <code>PreToolUse</code> hooks to auto-approve specific tools, or configure permission rules, which subagents <a href="/docs/en/sub-agents#permission-modes" class="link">inherit from the parent conversation</a>.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="recursive-hook-loops-with-subagents" aria-labelledby="_R_6v9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#recursive-hook-loops-with-subagents" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Recursive hook loops with subagents"></a><span id="_R_6v9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Recursive hook loops with subagents</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">A <code>UserPromptSubmit</code> hook that spawns subagents can create infinite loops if those subagents trigger the same hook. To prevent this:</span>
<ul>
<li>Use a shared variable or session state to track whether you’re already inside a subagent</li>
<li>Scope hooks to only run for the top-level agent session</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="systemmessage-not-appearing-in-output" aria-labelledby="_R_759deehkd74t9_"><a href="#systemmessage-not-appearing-in-output" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: systemMessage not appearing in output"></a><span id="_R_759deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">systemMessage not appearing in output</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">The <code>systemMessage</code> field shows a message to the user, not the model. On Claude Code v2.1.227 or later, a hook’s <code>systemMessage</code> can surface in the message stream as an <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#sdkinformationalmessage" class="link"><code>SDKInformationalMessage</code></a>. Whether it does depends on the event. Each <a href="/docs/en/hooks#hook-events" class="link">event’s section</a> on the hooks page says how output surfaces. To pass context to the model instead, return <a href="/docs/en/hooks#add-context-for-claude" class="link"><code>additionalContext</code></a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">Before v2.1.227, the SDK surfaced hook output in the message stream only for <code>SessionStart</code> and <code>Setup</code> hooks. For any other event, the output appeared only in the lifecycle events that <a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript#options" class="link"><code>includeHookEvents</code></a> (<code>include_hook_events</code> in Python) adds. That option’s entry covers which lifecycle events each hook event produces.</span>
<span data-as="p">If you need to surface hook decisions to your application reliably, log them separately or use a dedicated output channel.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="related-resources" aria-labelledby="_R_7d9deehkd74t9_"><a href="#related-resources" class="anchor-link" aria-label="Navigate to header: Related resources"></a><span id="_R_7d9deehkd74t9_" class="cursor-pointer">Related resources</span></h2>
<ul>
<li><a href="/docs/en/hooks" class="link">Claude Code hooks reference</a>: full JSON input/output schemas, event documentation, and matcher patterns</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/hooks-guide" class="link">Claude Code hooks guide</a>: shell command hook examples and walkthroughs</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript" class="link">TypeScript SDK reference</a>: hook types, input/output definitions, and configuration options</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/python" class="link">Python SDK reference</a>: hook types, input/output definitions, and configuration options</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions" class="link">Permissions</a>: control what your agent can do</li>
<li><a href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/custom-tools" class="link">Custom tools</a>: build tools to extend agent capabilities</li>

timeout`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Claude Code runs each callback with a timeout, which you set in seconds with the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`timeout`}),` field on its `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`HookMatcher`}),`. When you don’t set one, Claude Code uses the event’s default: 600 seconds for most events, 30 seconds for `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`UserPromptSubmit`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`PreModelSwitch`}),`, and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`PostModelSwitch`}),`, and 10 seconds for `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`MessageDisplay`}),`. Claude Code runs `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SessionEnd`}),` callbacks during shutdown under the shorter `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/hooks#sessionend-input`,children:`SessionEnd timeout budget`}),`, 1.5 seconds by default.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`When a callback exceeds its timeout, Claude Code cancels it and discards its output, and the session continues rather than hanging. What happens next depends on the event:`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`PreToolUse`}),`: Claude Code blocks the tool call. If every other hook returned nothing or only an `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`allow`}),` decision, the tool result tells Claude the hook didn’t respond before its timeout, and the turn continues. If another of your hooks already denied the call, its deny still reaches Claude: the tool result includes that hook’s reason, and the timeout text can follow it.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Before v2.1.210, Claude Code reported the timeout to Claude as a user rejection, which made unattended sessions stop and wait for input.`}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`PostToolUse`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`PostToolUseFailure`}),`: Claude Code keeps the tool result and the turn continues.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`UserPromptSubmit`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/hooks#userpromptexpansion`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`UserPromptExpansion`})}),`: Claude Code blocks the prompt with a message naming the hook and the timeout, and the session continues. Before v2.1.208, Claude Code ended the query with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`error_during_execution`}),` when a callback on these events timed out.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Stop`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`SubagentStop`}),`: the timed-out callback counts as returning no decision.
